# Action game (Dimraeth-like) — progress log

Owner direction 2026-11-08: a top-down pixel action RPG like Dimraeth, built around CLASSES (อาชีพ) instead of the legends.
Outfits change by recolouring (free); new outfit shapes later with PixelLab `transfer_outfit` (paid tier).
Reference clips the owner sent: Dimraeth trailer (one sword swing ≈ 5–6 poses at ~12 fps, characters ~32 px tall,
big white slash arcs drawn apart from the sprite) and a real-time monster-battle fan game (almost no character
animation, skills fire instantly as big effects, telegraph rings, a dodge button) — owner: "ไม่สวยแต่รวดเร็ว".

## Decisions
- Canvas 128 px (owner chose 128 after seeing 64 vs 128 side by side; figure ~118 px tall).
- 8 directions in the game. Default plan: animate 5 (south, south-east, east, north-east, north) and mirror the
  rest; mirroring puts a one-handed weapon in the other hand (owner told; not decided yet).
- First class: dagger rogue, a young woman. Rank 1 holds ONE dagger (right hand); rank 2 holds two (needs new moves).
- Normal attack = 2 hits (owner 2026-11-08).
- New characters: design in ChatGPT first (turnaround front / side facing right / back on white, neutral standing
  pose, arms a little away from the body, weapon held low), convert locally, show the owner, and only then PixelLab.
  Never start a PixelLab job without the owner's plain go.

## Files
- `art/incoming/action/rogue/turnaround_gpt.png` — the owner's GPT turnaround (1448×1086).
- `art/incoming/action/rogue/turnaround_cut.png` — white cut (`white_cut.py --edge-only --no-ring`).
- `art/incoming/action/rogue/px128/{south,east,north}.png` + `preview.png` — `tools/art/turn128.py` (one shared scale,
  figure 118 px tall, 32 colours, 1 px outline, feet 4 px above the bottom). south = front, east = side, north = back.

**Back views (N, NE, NW): ask for VERTICAL cuts** (overhead chop, rising slash) plus "seen from BEHIND … never turns … face never visible"; sideways swings make PixMiniMax turn her side-on or toward the camera.
Download the ZIP only between jobs (`/mcp/characters/<id>/download` answers "still being generated" while a job runs).

**Owner 2026-11-08 after the vertical redo: "ไปแก้ฟันบนทำไม เอาอันเก่าเลย … มันฟันไม่เหมือนกัน"** — the vertical chops
did not match the sideways slashes of the other directions, so the attack is the FIRST run (`attack_2hit_b`) in N, NE, NW
again (they turn a bit, accepted); the vertical chops are kept as `chop` (N + NW) for a skill later. Lower diagonals + W
still play the east clip. Build: `bash tools/art/rogue_build.sh` (writes `art/action/rogue.json` + one PNG per move).

Blade arcs: PixelLab painted each direction's arc its own colour (white, beige, cyan); `act_sheet.py smear()` repaints every
pixel that is not a colour of the stills and is light or bluish onto one cream-white ramp (owner "ทำสีให้เหมือนกันทุกทิศ").
Hit effects (owner "ใส่เอฟเฟคให้เหมือนเกมต้นแบบ"): pixel impact star → ring → sparkles at the hit point (`starFrames`, big on
the last hit), slime splat on a kill (`splatFrames`), a 16% white screen flash + camera kick toward the swing on the last hit,
run dust, roll after-images that copy the roll frame. Slash wave (owner "ทำคลื่นฟันให้สวยๆ ใช้กับทุกทิศ"): `waveFrames(dir, R)`
= a pixel crescent on the ground plane (y ×0.6) drawn per direction (no runtime rotation), 4 frames, white cutting edge, a
cream trail that fades out in Bayer dither, sparkles at the end; on every swing (`doHit`), bigger on the last hit.
Then (owner "ไม่ต้องทำมาซ้อน" + "สีฟันคนละสี ทำให้อยู่โทนเดียวกัน"): the code wave is OFF (kept in `waveFrames` for skills); the
arcs INSIDE the attack frames are made white instead: `act_sheet.py arcwhite()` (on a swing frame, light pixels outside the
nearby narrow frames' silhouettes grown 3 px, blobs ≥ 30 px, then grown into light pixels that were dark in the nearest
narrow frame, never into the top 30% = the head) + `smear()` (non-still colours), both onto one white ramp `SMEAR`.
**Longer dagger (owner asked): not possible in code** — the blade uses exactly the skin colours (246,215,191 / 214,185,159 /
173,147,123), so it cannot be picked out; a longer blade means regenerating every move (~50 credits). Plan: order longer
blades in the GPT design of rank 2 (dual daggers).

Glow + reach (owner sent a clip of strong glowing golden arcs, "สีแรงแสง", and "ระยะฟันมันสั้น"): `act_sheet.py --arc=air|gold|white`
picks the arc ramp; the attack gets `rogue_attack_glow.png` (arc pixels blurred) that the game adds with 'lighter'; a see-through
air blade (`airFrames`, colours from `META.arc`) flies on from each hit (760 / 900 px/s, damping .965) with a soft glow
(`glowSprite`/`glowAt`), white-warm glow on impacts; reach 180 / 230. Owner still to choose A gold or B air (built: air).
Bigger world (owner: "ไม่อยากลดตัวละคร เดี๋ยวเบลอ ก็ขยายแมพ สิ่งก่อสร้าง ให้ใหญ่ ให้ตัวละครดูเล็ก" — the 2x2-merge far camera
was tried and dropped): field 4200×2800; `makeProps` = sprite props drawn in the depth pass (fade to 45% when the rogue
stands behind) with baked ground shadows and solid bases (`blockProps`: ellipses, building footprints `rect`): huge trees
(`bigTree`, ~3× the rogue), boulders, tall ruined pillars, a hamlet of 4 cottages (`house`) and 2 ruined watchtowers (`tower`).

Then (owner): air blade OFF ("ไม่เอาใบมีดลม"), reach back to 125 / 150 × `META.k`; rogue shrunk to 80% at build time
("ย่อตัวลงหน่อย ... ตัวมันสูงไป", owner checked a 100%/80% comparison for blur and chose 80): `act_sheet.py --scale=4/5`
(each output pixel = the most common colour of the area it covers, a dark outline wins at ≥30%; cells padded to a multiple
of 5 → cell 140, feet 70/118, `k` 0.8 in the json); wider waves ("ทำคลื่นฟันให้กว้างกว่านี้"): two faint see-through bands
round every outside arc (3 and 6 px, the outer one dithered) and a wider glow.

Owner's play clip 2026-11-08: every J attack swung toward the mouse cursor left at the top while the slime came from below →
aim: a mouse click aims at the mouse, J / the touch button auto-aim at the nearest slime within 260 (else the walk direction);
the hit blink is now 45% see-through instead of vanishing. The wave bands looked like a leftover white frame → one 2 px band in
the arc's own edge colour/alpha.

Owner: "สีขาวตอนกดฟัน 2 เหมือนเอา bg ออกไม่หมด" → the 16% white screen flash on the last hit is gone (camera kick stays), the
slime hit/warning flash is a 55% white overlay instead of a solid white silhouette, the hit glow is smaller/softer (smoothed).

Owner's screenshot (two red lines either side of her while attacking): the glow layer's faint tail filled the whole cell and
stopped at its border → `act_sheet.py` now blurs the glow one cell at a time, cuts the faint tail (< 8%) to zero and fades
it out over the last 14 px before each cell edge (every cell border is 0).

A/B 2026-11-08: the owner's GPT sheet `gpt_nature.png` (oak, pine, blossom, bush, boulder, rocks, grass, flowers; prompt 1 in
this chat) → `white_cut.py --edge-only --no-ring` → `tools/art/props_px.py <cut> art/incoming/action/map/gpt_px 0=282 32
oak,pine,...` (one scale so the oak is 3× the rogue, each prop only its own blob, a palette per prop, 1 px outline) looks
clearly better than the PixelLab tree → map props from GPT. Owner on monsters: normal monsters frame by frame from GPT
like before (free); only bosses through PixelLab. **Decision (owner 2026-11-08 "เจน gpt แหละ pixellab เอาไว้ทำตัวละครหลังที่
เน้นควบคุมกับเอฟเฟค"): map + monsters from GPT; PixelLab only for the playable characters (controls) and skill effects.**
In the game: `art/action/props/<oak|pine|blossom|bush|boulder|rocks|grass|flowers>.png` replace the code trees / boulders /
pillars / grass clumps (`GPROP` in action.html, random horizontal flips); houses + towers stay code-drawn until the GPT
village sheet (prompt 2) arrives.

Ground + village (owner 2026-11-08): the 1-credit PixelLab standard tileset (grass ↔ dirt, 32 px) looked flat with stair-step
edges ("ไม่สวยเลย") → GPT ground textures `gpt_ground.png` (grass, dirt, sand, water, cobble, flower grass) →
`tools/art/ground_px.py <sheet> <out> 0.44 20 names` (scaled to the props' detail, made seamless by cross-fading with the
half-shifted copy, 20 colours; flower grass 40) → `art/action/ground/*.png`; `makeGround` samples them per pixel (`TEX`, `tx`)
times a big soft light/dark patch, ragged dithered edges between terrains, darker deep water, flower grass in the meadows;
the code ground is only a fallback. GPT village sheet `gpt_village.png` → `props_px.py ... 0=228 20 house_red,house_blue,
tower,well,fence,barrel,crate,lantern` → `art/action/props/`; houses / fences block as boxes (`GPDEF.rect`), houses are not
mirrored. Owner "เบาๆ ต้นไม้หน่อย": about half as many trees, bushes and grass clumps.

Bigger (owner 2026-11-08 "สิ่งก่อสร้างขยายได้อีกไหม", then "ให้ดูตัวละครเล็กลง ขยายแมพอีก มุมกล้องสูงอีก"): houses and the
tower re-cut 1.5× (`props_px.py ... 0=340`: house_red 330×342, house_blue 316×304, tower 272×418; footprints in `GPDEF`);
rogue 2/3 at build time (`rogue_build.sh --scale=2/3` → cell 116, feet 58/99, `k` .667; reach follows `META.k`); world
6000×4000 (`AR` = area vs the old 4200×2800, prop counts follow it; lake 1.35×, a longer stream, 7 meadows, 3 towers).
One 6000×4000 canvas is too big for phones, so the ground is painted in 512 px chunks (`CHUNKS`): `terr(x,y)` = what is at a
point (sets `TK`/`TA`/`TB`, path / stream widths cached per column / row in `PYC PWC SWC`, `streamD` skips far segments
`SEGB`), `groundPx` = its colour from the textures, `chunkStep` paints 16-row bands, then lily pads / reeds (`GND.deco`) and
the props' ground shadows (`PROPS[].shades`); `drawGroundChunks` draws what is on screen (paints a missing one at once) and
spends ~4 ms a frame on the nearest unpainted chunks in the ring round the view; far chunks are dropped above 60. The
walking water map `WATER` is worked out for the whole world in `setupGround`. Load paints only the start view (~0.5–1 s on
the PC); walking across the map: median 0.3 ms, 1 frame of ~675 over 16 ms. A "higher camera" can't be done with the
low-top-down art: the same feel comes from the smaller rogue (on a DPR-3 phone the screen shows 1170 world px of height,
she is ~1/17 of it).

Zones (owner 2026-11-08 "ทำแมพต่อ", free, existing art): `FORESTS` = two thick forests (north-east round the NE tower,
south-west; `forestF` > 0 inside): trees every ~118 px (pines first), bushes / rocks / grass under them, darker ground
(×.74, dithered edge), no flower grass, no random field trees near them; `RUINS` = broken cobble floor round the south tower
(kind 7: round grass patches from a sine pattern, dithered edge) ringed by boulders and rock piles; `SIDE` = 3 side paths
(Catmull-Rom `crv`, `sideD` with per-segment and per-path boxes) from the road to the three `TOWERS`; meadows moved out of
the forests. `ZF` = 32 px cells near a side path / forest, so most pixels skip those checks (`terr` 18 ms per chunk).
`?test=map` draws the whole world shrunk onto the screen (`drawMap`; picture `art/incoming/action/map/overview.png`).
Owner after playing: "เอาหญ้าออก ต้นไม้ใหญ่กว่านี้" → grass clumps gone (only flower clumps in the meadows); oak / pine /
blossom re-cut 1.5× (`props_px.py gpt_nature_cut.png <out> 0=423 20 oak,pine,blossom,bush,boulder,rocks,grass,flowers`,
only the three trees copied: oak 389×425, pine 275×446, blossom 274×324), trunk radii 30 / 24 / 22, edge trees every
320–470 px, forest trees ≥165 apart, field props ≥430 apart (the spacing, not the count, decides how thick the field is).
Town (owner 2026-11-08 pasted two GPT sheets: the ruins/forest/camp sheet from `PROMPT_ruins_forest.txt` and an
18-building sheet; pasted pictures come in the transcript as an `attachment` / `queued_command` line, saved as
`gpt_ruins.png` / `gpt_buildings.png`): ruins sheet `white_cut.py --no-ring` → `props_px.py ... 0=130 20
wall,wall_corner,arch,statue,pillar_down,pillar,rubble,stairs,stump,log,mushrooms,fern,dead_tree,mossy_rock,campfire,tent,
signpost,cart` (dead tree again at `12=330`) → `gpt_rpx/`; buildings sheet `white_cut.py --edge-only --no-ring`, then each
building moved into its own grid cell with gaps (`gpt_buildings_spaced.png`; neighbours touched and were read as one) →
`props_px.py ... 0=340 20 cottage_red,cottage_blue,cottage_thatch,guild,shop,healer,smithy,alchemist,library,armory,barn,
windmill,chapel,townhall,observatory,greenhouse,magetower,watermill` → `gpt_bpx/`; all copied to `art/action/props/`.
`GPDEF`: buildings `bld` (footprint 78% wide × 70, never mirrored), arch walk-through with a shadow (`shade`), dead tree
`tree` (canopy shade). Layout: `HAMLET` grown to 980 × 640 = a town round the well (`TOWN` slots: town hall, chapel,
library, shop, smithy, alchemist, guild, healer, armory, cottages; a slot too close to the road is skipped by
`roadClear`), a farm east of it (barn, windmill, greenhouse, thatched cottage), a watermill on the stream, a wizard's
corner north-west (mage tower, observatory); ruins `RUINS` 480 × 300: an arch where the side path comes in flanked by two
statues, walls, pillars, rubble, stairs, three dead trees round it; forests get ferns, mushrooms, stumps, logs, mossy rocks,
dead trees; a camp east of the lake (tent, fire, log, cart, barrel, crate); a signpost where each side path leaves the
road; fences keep out of buildings. Prompts waiting for the owner: `PROMPT_trees.txt` (16 trees), `PROMPT_thai_city.txt`
(old Thai capital: 18 buildings, 18 props, 6 ground tiles; attach `gpt_buildings.png`).
Earlier prompt: GPT sheet `art/incoming/action/map/PROMPT_ruins_forest.txt` (ruin walls, arch, pillars, rubble, stairs, statue,
stump, log, mushrooms, fern, dead tree, mossy rock, campfire, tent, signpost, cart; attach `gpt_village.png` for style).

## Skills (design in progress, owner 2026-11-08)
Proposed shape (owner asked "เอากี่สกิลดี"): 4 skill buttons on screen at once (room for thumbs round the attack button on a
phone) + attack + roll; about 8 skills per class to unlock up to level 50, the player picks which 4 to carry.
- **Skill 1 — throw dagger (owner):** throw at a spot on the ground (aimed area, not a target); if it hits a monster, the
  skill can be pressed again within 5 s → she appears BEHIND that monster. Built (free, no PixelLab): Q / 1 on PC (aims at
  the mouse), button ปา on phones (aims at the nearest slime in range, else straight ahead). `SK1` (range 380, area r 46,
  18 damage, stamina 18, cooldown 6 s, mark 5 s); the throw pose = the attack's second swing (owner "เอาคลื่นก็เหมือนปาแล้ว":
  `throwPlan` plays from 2 frames after the first hit to 4 after the last, the dagger leaves on the big arc; then owner
  "เอาคลื่นฟันออก": drawn from `makeNoArc(sheet)` = see-through arc pixels and the solid ramp colours removed, holes over
  the body filled from the neighbours, the arc's thin dark outline cut, no glow); `G.knives`
  fly in a slight arc with a ground shadow and stick in the ground; the monster nearest the landing spot gets `p.mark`
  (purple ring + arrow); the button turns purple "วาร์ป" / "Q!"; `blinkBehind` = after-image streak, purple puffs, she lands
  on the far side facing it (sides / stays put if that is water), .3 s i-frames. The flying dagger is a few code pixels
  (`drawKnife`) until the GPT dagger sheet arrives (`art/incoming/action/skills/PROMPT_dagger.txt` + `ref_rogue_dagger.png`:
  8 directions, 4 spin frames, 3 stuck-in-ground frames). Tests: `?test=sk1` (in flight), `?test=sk1b` (after the warp).
  Then (owner "มีดทำอันใหญ่กว่านี้นิด กำหนดระยะช่องสกิล มีดตอนปาทำแสดงด้วยจะได้เห็น"): aiming — hold Q (aims at the mouse)
  or press the ปา button and drag (70 css px = full range; a tap auto-aims), let go to throw; while aiming a dashed ring
  shows the longest throw, dots lead to a yellow circle where it lands (`AIM`, `aimStart`/`aimEnd`, `skTarget`,
  `ringSprite`, `?test=aim`); with the mark up a press warps at once. The flying dagger is ~30 px with a light streak and a
  soft glow, flies a little slower (900 px/s); the stuck one is bigger.
- **Skills 2–4 (owner 2026-11-08, "เจนมาเลยจาก pixellab"):** E / ปุ่ม หมุน = **spin slash** (PixelLab `spin`, south only —
  a spin looks the same from anywhere; played as wind-up, two turns with hits on both, recover; 2 × 13 to everything within
  108; she can drift; usable straight out of the throw or the warp = the owner's combo throw → warp behind → spin).
  R / ระเบิด = **smoke bomb** (PixelLab `bomb`, E S N NW, rest mirrored/aliased; aimed like skill 1, range 340; a high lob,
  blast r 96, 12 damage, **blind** 4 s: blind slimes hop at random, aim their lunges off and miss half the time; grey smoke
  circles their heads; `smokeSprite` cloud). F / พุ่ง = **flurry** (owner asked whether a vanishing move needs 8
  directions — no: no new moves at all): aim at a monster (range 300, red ring on it), she vanishes and cuts through it
  6 times 150° apart (`streak` fx + crescent + attack-frame after-images), 6 × 9 + 8, invulnerable, moves on to a monster
  close by if it dies, lands behind it. Cooldowns 5 / 9 / 10 s; costs 15 / 20 / 25 stamina. Phone: 4 skill buttons in an
  arc round ตี / กลิ้ง; PC: Q E R F (or 1–4) with squares under the bars. Tests `?test=spin|bomb|bomb2|flurry`.
  Spin redone (owner "หมุนตัวไม่สวย ... ดูแข็งไม่มีพลัง", "ให้มีระยะกว้างหน่อย"): PixelLab's spin only turned half way and played twice
  looked like twisting, so it is no longer used: she turns through the 8 directions twice (`SK2.step` 30 ms, the attack's
  swing frame of each direction with its arc + glow, after-images), wind-up 70 ms, settle 90 ms, hits at the end of each
  turn, reach 140; a PixelLab VFX `spin_slash` ring (size 2.8, air colours; My effects "rogue_spin_ring" 034322c3…,
  game pack → `fx/spinring_b.png` behind her + `fx/spinring_f.png` in front, `drawPfx` with `follow`/`depth`) at the
  start of each turn; a ring of dust at the feet. `?test=spin&n=<steps>`.
  Owner "เอฟเฟกต์ทำใสหน่อย มีแสง": `fx/<key>_glow.png` = a blurred, tinted copy of an effect (12 px padding, light cyan;
  lavender for nslash) for spinring_b / spinring_f / nslash; `drawPfx` draws such effects at 78% and adds the glow with 'lighter'.
- **Skill 4 redone like Murad's ultimate in ROV** (owner: "สกิล 4 ไม่สวยเลย" + a GPT pose sheet, then a clip of Murad, "พุ่งฟัน 5 ที"):
  aim at a spot (range 320); a see-through cyan field opens on the ground there (`fieldSprite`, an ellipse r 140 × .6, four
  opening steps, glow, glints round the rim); she dashes across it 5 times in a pentagram (`nextCut`, 144° apart) with the
  PixelLab `dash` move (generated 2026-11-08: E S N NW, group 1c6f5ef3-04e7-4e97-97c4-93dfbab67f9f, 4 generations; only the
  side view kept its facing, so every direction plays east / mirrored: crouch 2, lunge 3–4, slash 5 with its arc + glow,
  follow-through 6), after-images on the way, a cyan streak per pass, every monster in the field hit each pass (5 × 9),
  then a cyan burst in the middle (16, VFX `crit_hit` recoloured cyan = `fx/dashhit.png`) and she ends at the edge; a
  VFX `charge_up` (`fx/dashcharge.png`) at the start. Invulnerable throughout.
- Bomb blast (owner "ระเบิดเปลี่ยนเอฟเฟกต์ ใส่ระเบิดกับควันให้เข้าสกิล ตาบอด"): VFX `cartoon_boom` + a dark `smoke_puff`, ×1.85,
  1.5 s (My effects "rogue_smoke_bomb") → `fx/smokebomb.png` via `tools/art/vfx_import.py`.
- Cuts (owner "ไม่เอาเส้นตรง" → "รอยฟันแบบรอยผ่า"): skill 4 puts a big cyan VFX `cross_slash` (`fx/xcut.png`, size 2.6) on every
  monster each pass; the spin throws crescents (`slash_trail`, cyan, 8 facings × 3 sizes: `dslashs*` 1.5, `dslash*` 2.3,
  `dslashl*` 3.3; `slashFor(deg, size)` picks the one that bulges that way) in three layers round her every other step
  (owner's picture: 3–4 overlapping swirls of different sizes). Then (owner "ซ้อนกันให้ได้วงกลม วงละ 90 องศา"): every 4 steps a
  full circle = 4 crescents bulging out 90° apart, two sizes at once (mid + large, the large a beat later), each set turned
  45° from the last; 55% see-through with a weaker glow, only the arc nearest the camera in front of her, so her body shows. Made with `tools/pl_vfx.py <export> <key> '<part json>'`
  (start → add_part → export → download → `vfx_import.py`, all free); `_glow` copies for each.
  Dash lightning (owner asked for a straight dash line with lightning chasing it; their own VFX Creator try was lost unsaved):
  VFX `dash_slash` (cyan, 2.4) + two own `bolt` layers along it (jag .07, 2 branches, growing back to front, flickering) +
  a spark burst, My effects "rogue_dash_bolt" → `fx/dashbolt.png` (orange tip sparks recoloured) + mirrored `dashbolt_l`;
  skill 4's passes now cross the field left / right in turn with a slight slant (`nextCut`), one streak per pass.
  Then the owner's own VFX Creator effect "Cyan lightning dash" (My effects 3997ec67-2cf4-49f9-ba5d-caeab4cd24b0, made with
  the prompt above, Pro = 4 generations) replaced it: opened with `start --effect <id>` (the id was read from the VFX
  Creator page's "Open" button, `vfx-library-open-<id>`, after the owner signed in in the built-in browser), ×1.7, exported
  as "rogue_lightning_dash_game" → `fx/dashbolt.png` + mirrored `dashbolt_l` + glows.
  Owner "เอารอยฟันเก่าออก": the X cuts are gone; "เจนรอยผ่าดูเผื่อสวย 1 เครดิตเอง" → generated in the VFX Creator chat in the
  built-in browser (Lite, 1 generation): "Cyan sword cut" (3b257bb0-20fd-43d8-9dbb-3554b2f0e459: a thin diagonal gash, flash,
  sparks, splits apart) ×1.6 → `fx/swordcut.png` + mirrored `swordcut_m` + glows, on every monster each pass (alternating).
  Then (owner "ให้ดูเหมือนรอยผ่า") a second Lite run in the same chat: a lens-shaped wound with a dark split, flash and sparks,
  saved as new (My effects 1aefbc42-0270-479d-9cfd-222c00743d9c) ×1.4 → `fx/woundcut.png` + `woundcut_m`, used instead of the thin
  cut. Then (owner "เปลี่ยนสกิลให้เขียวเรืองแสงสวยๆ ทั้งสกิลเลย") the whole of skill 4 is glowing green: `<key>_g` sheets of
  dashbolt / dashbolt_l / woundcut / woundcut_m / dashhit / dashcharge (each pixel moved onto a green ramp by its brightness)
  + green glows, the field sprite's colours, the field glow, the hit sparks, a green glow on her while she dashes.
  Owner then "เปลี่ยนสีฟ้าดีกว่า": back to cyan-blue (the `_g` sheets stay in fx/, not loaded).
  Then "ไม่ฟ้าแบบนี้ ฟ้าแบบน้ำเงินสายฟ้า": electric blue `_b` sheets (ramp #0a1248 #1c3cd2 #4080ff #a5cdff #f5faff, glow 70,130,255) for
  every skill-4 effect, the field and her glow — current.
  **Skill 4 as a STAR** (owner: "เอฟเฟกต์ฟันไม่สวย เอาออก ให้ฟัน 5 ที เป็นรูปดาว", "ทำวงสวยๆ เพิ่มเอฟเฟกต์", "ให้ดูหนักหน่วง รุนแรง"):
  no cut marks, no horizontal streak; a spinning VFX `rune_circle` (5 points, electric blue, `fx/starrune.png`, drawn at 50%
  under the bodies by `drawField`, field 160 × .42); she blinks to the top point and dashes the pentagram (`STAR` order
  0 2 4 1 3 0), each line stays on the ground as crackling light (`drawStarLines`: re-jagged every 2 frames, dark edge / blue /
  light / white core, glow), a VFX `bolt_strike` (`fx/starbolt.png`) at every point she reaches, everything inside hit per
  line (5 × 9); `starFinish`: the star flashes, five strikes together, VFX `shockwave_ring` (`fx/starshock.png`), a burst,
  22 damage, hit-stop .14, shake 11, camera kick.
  Owner "ไม่สวย เอาดาวออก": the rune circle re-made without its hexagram / star ring (My effects "rogue_rune_nostar" →
  `fx/starrune.png`), and each dash line now only flashes (.22 s after the dash) instead of staying, so no star builds up;
  the five dashes, lightning strikes and the heavy finish stay.
  Owner's picture of "Cyan sword cut" recoloured blue in VFX Creator, "ใส่อันนี้ ให้ทิ้งรอยไว้ หน่วง" + "ใช้แทนพุ่งสายฟ้าเลย":
  3b257bb0 re-exported ×2.8 ("rogue_big_cut"), pixels moved onto the owner's blue (sampled from their screenshot: #081a46
  #0d5694 #0fc3ff #83f4f9 #f0fcff), the clean-line frame held 9 frames (~0.65 s) with a slow fade, then turned offline

  lightning line, no strikes per dash (the finish keeps five strikes + shockwave). `fx/bluecut*` = the smaller held cut.
  Owner "ท่าจบ ลบเส้นสีขาวออก": the dash is drawn from an arc-free copy (`noArcOf(sheet)` = `makeNoArc`, cached per sheet,
  also for the dash after-images) and skips frame 5 (the one with PixelLab's big slash arc): lunge 3–4, then 6.
  Owner: "เอาสายฟ้าตอนจบออก" → no lightning strikes at the finish (shockwave + burst stay); then "สายฟ้าพุ่งในภาพ แทนรอยฟัน"

  Then owner "ไม่ต้องใช้อะไรเลย ตัวมันพุ่งเป็นเงารางๆ สีฟ้าก็พอ" + "ใส่ฟ้าให้เงา ให้เห็นเงามันด้วย": no effect on the dashes at all;

  Then the owner's Zenitsu clip ("เอาแบบนี้", "เอาแค่สกิลพุ่ง"): she vanishes and zips rim to rim in a zigzag (`rimPt`, each

- **New heroes + monster brief** (owner 2026-11-09 "ทำพรอมต์สร้างตัว ดาบชาย เมจชาย", "คำสั่งเจนมอนเป็นชุด จะให้ Codex ทำ"):
  the owner made both heroes in GPT from my prompts (pixel-style turnarounds, front / right profile / back, both correct):
  `art/incoming/action/swordsman/turnaround_gpt.png` (2000x667 as pasted, greatsword on the right shoulder, red scarf) and
  `art/incoming/action/mage/turnaround_gpt.png` (1448x1086, indigo hood + robe, teal cape, staff with a teal crystal).
  The figures are different sizes only because GPT drew them on different canvases; they are scaled to one height when
  they go into PixelLab / the game. PixelLab characters for them wait for the owner's go (credits).

  kb 40+ or a kill throws a light monster off its feet (`m.kz` height, `m.kvz`, gravity 1200, no walking while in the air, less
  friction in the air, lands with dust + a ring), bosses and stout ones only slide; dying bodies keep sliding / flying and still
  stop at walls and water; skid dust while sliding fast; every hit an impact star (`hstar`, `drawHitStar`) and a kick of dust,

- **One view for every screen** (owner 2026-11-09 "ให้เห็นเท่ากัน ไม่งั้นเสียเปรียบ เอาเท่าที่ส่งมาให้ดู", replaces the phone zoom
  below): every screen shows the same `FVW` x `FVH` = 1266 x 585 world px (the 2x phone view). `onResize`: `S` = whole canvas
  px per world px (ceil of fit x DPR), `Q` = canvas px per CSS px (= DPR on a 3x phone, finer elsewhere, then the browser
  scales the canvas down smoothly: `image-rendering` auto unless Q = DPR), `OX`/`OY` = where the view starts; other screen
  shapes get dark bars (PC 16:9 thin top/bottom, 4:3 tablets big ones). Mouse → world = (mouse * Q - OX) / S + cam; the
  screen-space overlay uses `setTransform(Q)`. Owner then: "ไม่ได้บอกคมเท่ากัน หมายถึงระยะมองเห็นเท่ากัน" → every screen draws at its
  own pixels (`Q` = DPR, `S` = fit x DPR, a whole number on a 3x phone, 1.33 on a 2x phone, 1.52 on a 1080p PC; the world
  offset is rounded to whole pixels); `?sharp=1` brings back the finer whole-S drawing scaled down by the browser.

  stamina). Mage (`MGS`): two homing bolts per attack (`mageBolt`, `G.pshots`), Q fireball (burn), E chain lightning,
  R frost nova (`m.frz`), F blink (move direction), Z meteor (`G.meteors`, telegraph ring, 1 s), X mana shield (`p.msh`,
  bursts into lightning when broken); passive elements (`applyEl` / `react`: steam, shatter, overload, floating Thai text
  `G.tags`). Mob statuses: `m.stun`, `m.frz` (blue tint), `m.burn`. Test `?test=hsk&hero=..&k=0..6&n=steps[&rel=]`.
  Codex works in the same folder and switches branches, so Claude now works in a separate worktree
  (`C:\last-signal-pvp-claude`, branch `claude/work`, preview "game-claude" on port 8001) and pushes to main.

## Thai map (owner 2026-11-08 chose "a separate map")
`action.html?map=thai`: walk off the east end of the first map's road (signpost + "เมืองไทย →" label) → fade, hp / stamina /
kills carried in sessionStorage (`act.carry`), `?map=thai&from=west` starts at the west end of the Thai road; its west end
leads back (`travel`, `spawnAt`). Same chunk ground with the owner's riverside textures (`TEXF`: rv_dirt road, rv_mud shore,
rv_water, planks for the bridge deck, reeds in the paddies) and layout per map (`TH`): a wide river (`SWC` 118 ± 16,
`riverX`) through the middle with a plank bridge, a walled royal city west of it (sema walls, four forts, two gates on the
road, palace, temple, stupa, bell tower, sala, library on the lotus pond, lions, flags, elephant statues, houses, smithy, tea
house, elephant stable, a market of stalls along the road, Thai lanterns), stilt houses standing in the river on both banks
with kitchens, granaries, wells, jars behind, boats and lotus on the river, a temple on the east bank, rice paddies with
dikes (`PADDY`, kind 8) with a watchtower, buffalo pen, granary, huts, an ox cart, prang ruins in the north-east jungle,
jungle groves of coconut / sugar palms / bananas / rain trees (`thaiProps`). Pieces: the owner's four GPT sheets (`gpt_thai_*`)
→ `white_cut.py --edge-only --no-ring`, spaced into a 6×3 grid → `tools/art/thai_props.py` (heights per item, water cut in
the full-size sheet under stilt houses before the colours are reduced) and `ground_px.py ... 0.44 20 rv_dirt,rv_mud,
rv_water,planks,straw,reeds`. Overview: `action.html?map=thai&test=map`.

### Map fixes (owner 2026-11-08 "แก้แมพต่อ บางอย่างแบ็คกราวด์เอาออกไม่หมด เงาลอย ไปทำน้ำ ไม่ต้องรออนุญาต")
- Leftover white background between posts: flat pure-white patches (> 60 px, min channel > 225, chroma < 16) plus their pale
  fringe cut from v_pen, v_well, t_stall, v_kitchen, v_pavilion, t_sala, v_stall, v_granary, v_watchtower, v_bridge,
  bridge_curve. NOT from t_palace, t_temple, v_stupa, t_stupa, t_gate, t_belltower, v_temple, shop, chapel (their own white
  walls / awning stripes; checked by marking the patches in magenta).
- Floating: most GPT props have 4–10 empty rows under their base, so they stood above their shadows → `loadProps` finds each
  picture's lowest opaque row (`GPROP[n].foot`) and `gp` pins it (fy = foot - 2); the crown shade (`ao`) only under oak /
  pine / blossom / rain tree, smaller and on the ground round the trunk (on palms and bananas it hung mid-trunk).
- Water: the Thai map uses the clear blue `water` texture (the muddy `rv_water` looked dull grey); foam laps at every shore on
  both maps (`SHORE_PTS` = water cells next to land with the way to land, drawn in `drawGround`: a short white / pale line
  washing toward the land and back, a slow wave along the shore); twice the sparkles on the Thai river.
- Thai city floor: `art/action/ground/laterite.png` = the cobble texture's brightness on an old red laterite / brick ramp
  (#4a261e … #d69668, 20 colours), used for the Thai map's yard instead of the grey European cobbles.

## Game: `action.html` (prototype, 2026-11-08)
One file. Bright meadow painted in code at 1 world px = 1 art px (`makeGround`: diamond tiles, grass tones with Bayer
dither, dirt path, tufts, flowers, rocks, bushes, a ring of trees), whole-number screen scale `S` (about 560 world px of
height on screen). Sprite sheet `art/action/rogue.png` + `.json` from `tools/art/act_sheet.py` (row 0 = 8 stills, then the
attack per direction; `hits` per direction). Rogue: 8-direction walk (still picture + 2 px bob, no run animation yet),
attack = the direction's own frames, hits from the json (2 hits 12+20, 3 hits 10+10+15), hit-stop, shake, sparks,
pixel damage digits, pixel arc; roll (Space / right click / K) 0.32 s with i-frames and after-images; stamina (attack 15,
roll 26, regen after 0.6 s). Mouse aims on PC; on phones a left-side stick, buttons ตี / กลิ้ง and auto-aim to the nearest
slime. Slimes (code-drawn pixel sprites, green / blue from wave 3) hop, then warn (flash + red ring) and lunge.
`?test=atk&dir=N` freezes on the last hit for screenshots (headless Edge: `msedge --headless=new --screenshot=...`).
Next: run animation, roll animation, redo N/NE attacks, then levels / map (owner asked about a leveling map).

  `docs/berserker.md` (passive + Q chain gather → 360 cleave, E triple cleave, R charged ground slam, F chain hook dash, Z chain

- **Sharp pixels everywhere** (owner 2026-11-10 "ภาพมันไม่ชัด" → "เอาไม่ย่อ" → "เอาฉากให้คม"): the blur came from scaling pixel art by
  fractions (heroes shrunk 2/3 at build time, then the whole world drawn ×1.52 on a 1080p PC). Now: heroes use PixelLab's own
  size (`art/action/n128/`, `N128=1 bash tools/art/hero_build.sh <hero>` / `rogue_build.sh`), drawn at a whole number of screen
  px per art px (`drawCell`); the world is drawn at S = a multiple of 1.5 (`ARX` 1.5 art px per world px, `onResize`; the view
  follows the screen, ≈1280×640 on a PC, 1.5 on most phones), and everything in it has 1.5× pictures drawn 1 art px per screen
  px: ground chunks painted at 1.5× (`chunkStep`, textures `art/action/ground_hr/` from `ground_px.py ... 0.66`, laterite from
  the cobble brightness), props `art/action/props_hr/` (`PROP_K=1.5` in `rebuild_props.py` / `thai_props.py` +
  `tools/art/props_hr_extra.py` for the wall / fort pieces; gates = props_px on the cut `thai_gate_4view.png` at 0=570;
  `drawProp` pins the hr picture at the low-res one's bottom centre), monsters `art/action/mobs_hr/` (`MOB_K=1.5 mob_frames.py`,
  `MOBS[k].d`; frost_wolf has no hr sheet, its see-through source frames are not in the repo). Effects are still scaled.
  `?ar=0` = the old soft fit, `?n128=0` = the old shrunk heroes, `?zoom=N` / `?hz=N` = zoom tests.
- **Swordsman Q = the owner's ParticleFX fire line** (2026-11-10, pasted 9x8 sheet on see-through, 66 frames, raw
  `art/incoming/pfx/fire-line-sheet.webp`; "ไปใช้คู่กับท่า 1 ดาบ ละทำช่องสกิลเป็นแนวยาว"): cut on a 222x79 grid → `fx/pfx_fireline.png`
  (manifest `len` 133 = the line x 35..168 at y 50, anchor at its near end). `groundSplit` lays it along the crack (k 'esplit' with
  `key`, from frame 0, scaled to the crack's length, height ×.85 of that), hits land as the fire runs out (t × .55 s); the
  Earthsplitter sheet stays as the fallback. Skills with `line: 1` (SWS[1]) draw a straight band as their aim marker (the
  crack's reach and width) instead of the range ring + target circle. Its skill icon uses the fire line too.
- **Norse monsters in** (Codex, branch `codex/mobs-china`, 2026-11-10): draugr hurt + troll, ice_wraith, dwarf_berserker, ice_golem,
  boss_jotun (walk / attack / death / hurt) → `mob_frames.py` + `MOB_K=1.5` (both sheets); `?test=mobs&set=norse` shows all seven
  with the boss. Queue `docs/codex/action/MOB_ANIM.md` copied from Codex's branch (172 rows done). frost_wolf keeps its low-res
  sheet (its see-through source frames are not in the repo).
- **Rogue 3-hit combo** (owner 2026-11-10 with Ozzbit Games' free hero template zip, "คอมโบเค้าสวยมาก ใช้กับมีดเราได้ไหม" → "คอมโบ 3";
  that pack is personal / non-commercial only, so nothing of it is used, only the idea): `atkPlan` gives the rogue's normal
  attack a frame list `fr` = the sheet's two swings, then its first swing again as a finisher (hits 10 / 11 / 17); each hit
  calls `rogueCres` = a crescent drawn in code (`drawCres`: fat white middle, neon rim, dark edge, squashed to the ground
  angle, the tail eaten toward the tip after 25%, sparkles off the tip), alternating forehand / backhand, the finisher
  bigger; red during rage.
- **Move-copy tool** (owner 2026-11-10 "ทำทูลขึ้นมาสำหรับการลอกท่า ... ให้ใช้ในโทรศัพท์ได้"): copies an example animation's POSES onto
  our own character (no pixels of theirs). Steps: PixelLab `estimate_skeleton` on every example frame (small figures are cropped
  and enlarged first, the 256 px limit) and on our still (0.1 each) → retarget: each of our bones takes the example bone's
  angle and keeps our length, built out from our hips; our own face rides on the neck → `animate_with_skeleton_v3`
  (`first_frame_url` = our still, `first_frame_keypoints`, view low top-down; 3 frames 2 gen, 4-14 = 3, 15 = 4), results from
  `GET /mcp/images/<job>/download?index=N`. Command line: `tools/art/pose_copy.py <frames dir | sheet.png[:cols]> <still.png>
  <out> "<looks>" [action] [direction] [--dry]`. Phone page: `posecopy.html` (GitHub Pages; the owner's PixelLab key is typed
  in and kept in that browser's localStorage only; PixelLab's API allows calls from boxchaychan.github.io): example file(s) →
  hero + direction from `art/action/n128/<hero>_still.png` (or an own picture) → อ่านท่า (preview of both skeletons) →
  สร้างท่า (plays the result, saves a one-row PNG). First test: Ozzbit's combo_1 + end (7 frames) on the rogue, east, 3 gen
  → `art/incoming/action/rogue/pose_copy_ozzbit/` (low lunge with the dagger forward, back to standing; she keeps her look).
- **Lessons from the owner's sample idle / walk** (2026-11-10 `sampleidlewalk.zip`, hand-made 64 px Aseprite files, not kept in
  the repo): idle 10 frames at 60 ms (the body barely moves, the hair and cloak sway and lag = secondary motion), walk 26 frames
  at 50 ms with a turn-around inside it, a 2-frame "from idle" link into the walk, 26-27 colours. Taken into the game (free):
  heroes turn through the in-between directions (`p.vd`, a step every 45 ms; a half-turn passes the front view), idle / run
  start from their first frame when the state changes (`p.stT0`), idle 7 → 9 fps. For future PixelLab idles / runs: ask for
  hair and cape swaying and lagging behind, more frames (12-16) where it matters.
- **Guardian moves copied from a knight pack** (owner 2026-11-10, "FREE Knight 2D Pixel Art v1.2": games OK incl. commercial, no
  redistribution as an asset, so its sheets are NOT in this repo; frames 112x96): `pose_copy.py` ATTACK 1 (8) / 2 (9) / 3 (11) /
  BLOCK (6) onto the Guardian's east rotation (paid character 4577584b…), 3 gen each = 12 → `art/incoming/action/guardian/
  pose_copy_knight/<move>/r*.png` + `preview.gif`: overhead cut into a lunge, a thrust, a low lunge cut, shield raised.

## Map 1 walk test (2026-11-11)
Owner "แมพเอาให้เสร็จก่อน1แมพละฉันจะลองเดินดู": `map.html` = walk-only test of one full top-down map (1800×1100 map px, hero 50 px,
about 400 map px tall on screen, blown up by a whole number). Built for free by `python3 tools/art/td_map.py` → `art/td/map1/`
(ground.png with cliffs / stairs / prop shadows baked in, props.png atlas, block.png = where you cannot walk at half size, hero and
monster sheets shrunk to map size, map.json) + `art/td/map1_preview.png`. Two plateaus (top band + an island) with stairs, a pond,
the 4 PixelLab rocks (moss added), forest edges, olive grade. Controls: WASD / arrows, touch = hold and drag; `?dbg` or B shows the
blocked area. Monsters only wander for now. No fighting yet.
In the game (owner 2026-11-11 "โอเคเดิยระบบตีเลย"): `action.html?map=td1` (first button in the start menu, also on the town's warp
list) plays this map with the full fight system. `TDM` / `TDK` (= 4/3 world px per map px, so 2 art px: whole screen pixels at every
S): WW × WH 2400 × 1467, the walk grid `WATER` (4 px cells) comes from block.png, `drawGroundChunks` draws ground.png, props come from
the atlas (`q.td`, all `soft`: the block picture already holds their footprints), tree crowns `TD.over` are drawn over the bodies,
camps = the map's `spawn` groups, roster `ROSTER.td1`. Tested: stairs up, cliffs and the pond block, auto fight, 60 fps.
td1 view (owner 2026-11-11 "ภาพจอก็ไม่เท่ากับเทส"): `onResize` gives td1 the map.html view (~400 map px tall, K = whole screen px
per map px, S = K / TDK); the hero is then 2/3 world px per art px and, when K is odd (`TDS`), hero and monsters are drawn smooth.
A ⛶ button (`#fsBtn`, under the score) toggles fullscreen on every map (start() only asks for it on touch when the start was a tap).
td1 vs map.html (owner 2026-11-11 "ทำไมคนละระดับ" + two screenshots): the Berserker's v4 side-view sheets (bz4, figure 135 art px)
were drawn at a whole screen px per art px = ~90 CSS px tall, almost twice the walk test; td1 now draws him at .54 world px per art
px (~73 world px like the other heroes), monsters scaled by `TDMS` to the walk test's sizes, and the walk test's dark edges are drawn.
Owner 2026-11-11 "แมพเก่าเอาออกเลย เริ่มแมพพิกเซลใหม่": td1 is the default and only map in the menu (map row hidden, warp entry
gone); the old maps are kept in the code and still open with ?map=west|thai|jungle|town.
Moves per hero at that point (own directions, the rest mirrored/borrowed): run 8 dirs for all but the Guardian (side view only) and
the Berserker's v4 look (side view, left/right); attacks: swordsman / mage / priest 5, archer 6, rogue 4 (south-east borrows east),
Guardian 1 (side), Berserker v4 side view (sweep, vertical, spin, chain, leap). Missing: Guardian and Berserker run/attack facing
up and down.

## Map 2: autumn forest (2026-11-12)
Owner "ทำแมพรอไปก่อน" (while the Assassin picture waits): `MAP=td2 python3 tools/art/td_map.py` → `art/td/map2/` + `map2_preview.png`:
grass with carpets of fallen red / orange leaves (the autumn tileset over the pro grass), a river from top to bottom with a plank
bridge where the road crosses (rails block), one plateau with stairs in the north-east, trees / bushes recoloured to autumn (`autumn()`:
green hues → red-orange patches), the big rocks, props placed at random free spots (`spot`). map.json now has `exits` (boxes in map
px → another map) and `entries` (where you arrive from a map); the border is opened where the road crosses an exit box. Map 1's east
road end ↔ map 2's west road end. action.html: `?map=td2` (MAP), files from `art/td/map2/`, `ROSTER.td2` (skeleton, wolf, wisp,
shroom, goblin archer, boar guard, golem), exit labels drawn on the road, `spawnAt` uses `entries[FROM]`, travel() carries hp / level.
(map.html stays map 1 only; td2 has no hero / monster sheets of its own.)
Props set 2 in the maps (2026-11-12): `tools/art/props_cut.py <raw> <dir> <name> [--grid=CxR]` cut the 11 pictures into 70 pieces
(`art/td/props/set2/cut/`; the trees cleaned by hand of drawn shadows / leaf litter); `td_map.py` adds them on both maps with
`spot2` (random free spots; `gap` between pieces of one call, `own` from everything else; `NEAR` = beside the road): extra trees
(green on map 1, autumn on map 2), a camp (tent, fire, crates, barrels, sacks), lamp posts / a well / hay / a bench (map 1) or a
signpost (map 2) by the road, ruins (on map 1's plateau, round map 2), logs and stumps, bushes, stones, lily pads in the pond /
river, reeds on the banks, a rowing boat on map 2's river. `magic2` is not used.
Hand-placed maps (owner 2026-11-12 "ของจริงใส่มั่วไปหมด" after the showcase scenes `tools/art/td_showcase.py`): `td_map.py` now keeps
only the edge forest from the code placement and puts every inside prop by hand in themed groups (`H_(kind, name, h, x, y)`, nudged up
to 56 px off road / water / cliffs / monster camps, else dropped and printed). Map 1: a camp south-west of the road, the crossroads
(lamps, signpost, bench, well, hay), a fishing pond (lily pads, reeds, bench, lamp, willow), ruins on the island and the north ridge,
a grove of logs in the south-east. Map 2: a camp in the north-west clearing, lamps at both ends of the bridge, reeds / lily pads / a
boat along the river, temple ruins round the south road, dead wood east of the river, ruins on the plateau.
Owner 2026-11-12 ("พุ่มไม้ไปอยู่ในน้ำได้", "บันได...ไม่ได้มีเนิน", "ของตกแต่งกับใช้งานแยกไม่ออก", "โคมไฟในป่าเข้ากันไหม"): the wild maps
now hold only nature and ancient ruins: `SKIP` in td_map.py drops every camp_* / village_* piece (lamps, benches, well, hay, tent,
crates, signs), the stone-stairs ruin and the reeds in the water; fallen logs / mushrooms / stones fill the old camp spots. Village
things are for the town map later.
Assassin v2 in the game (owner 2026-11-12 "ลองเอาเข้าแมพยัง"): `tools/art/as2_build.py` → `art/action/as2/` (cell 196x152, feet
70,146; run 14 frames, attack 9 = the left thrust + the upward slash with PixMiniMax's pink swooshes / dust removed, stills side /

smoothing blurred it. "Sharp bilinear" now: `as2Sharp(img, k)` caches the sheet blown up by a whole number with hard pixels, and only
that copy is shrunk a little with smoothing (crisp edges, no blur). Used by the Assassin (`drawAs2`), every hero cell (`drawCell`
when `TDS`), the Berserker v4 on td maps, and the monsters (`drawMob2` when `TDS`). 58-60 fps on the phone test.

84,144): 8 stills, run (the south-east run stands in for the side, whose arms came out grey; north / north-east keep their matching
half), slash combo and crit spin in east / south-east / south (one turn kept). PixMiniMax's swooshes / X flash / dust are removed by
colour: anything far from her own palette (the colours of her 8 stills) plus its rim, loose pieces, dust at the feet; frames that were
mostly effect or lost their legs behind a swoosh are dropped; black outline. action.html `AS3` / `drawAs3` (used by `drawAs2` once
loaded): left-hand directions mirrored, upward attacks use the side view, the crit (every 4th hit, `p.pcHit`) and the E whirl play
the spin. Menu card = her south still.
Owner 2026-11-12 "วงไว้ทำตอนสกิล ... ทำไมจะลบเอฟเฟกต์": the spin clip is kept untouched as a second row `spinfx` (PixMiniMax's own glowing
rings) for the E whirl skill; the old rogue whirl ring and after-images are off for her. The crit after normal hits keeps the clean spin.


### Dogs vs cats mocks (2026-11-12, free)
`tools/art/tw_ld_mock.py` (Lucky-Defense-style portrait, two boards) was made more even (owner "ทำให้มันสมส่วนหน่อย"): cells 52x70 with round stone discs, the shiba 60 px fills its cell, monsters 36 px, the loop closer to the grid, a smaller middle panel. `tools/art/tw_cam_mock.py` → `art/incoming/dogcat/tw_cam_mock.png` (owner "ลองใช้มุมกล้องหลายๆแบบให้ฉันเทียบดู"): the same board from 4 cameras: A straight top-down, B high 3/4 (ground ×.75), C low 3/4 (×.5), D isometric (turned 45°, ×.5, shrunk to fit 390 px); the ground is painted flat and squashed / turned per camera, characters stay upright at 1:1, slots get a thicker front face as the camera drops. Findings: A shows the slots best but the characters cover the row above; C and D crowd the 6x3 grid on a portrait phone; B is the LD-like middle.
Stone floor (owner 2026-11-12 "ทำพื้นเป็นบล็อกหิน", free): `tools/art/stone_px.py` `blocks()` paints pixel stone blocks in code (mortar, lit top / left edge, darker front lip, specks, cracks, moss). In `tw_ld_mock.py` each board is now a raised stone platform: grey cobble loop, light sandstone slabs lined up with the slot grid, a low step where the middle meets the road, a mossy stone wall under the front edge with a shadow on the grass; slots are raised stone pedestals (4-tile top + front face). The shiba in the mock is now the PixelLab character's south view at 1:1.
Shrinking the shiba (owner 2026-11-12 "ถ้าย่อชิบะจะเบลอไหม", free): `tools/art/shiba_shrink.py` → `art/incoming/dogcat/shrink/cmp_x4.png`: original 61 px, plain resize to 46 smooth (blurry rim) and nearest (lines broken / doubled, face uneven), the 2x2 merge to 30 px (sharp but the face and kimono pattern mostly gone). Clean small sizes need PixelLab at that size (1 credit), or scale the whole screen instead.
Zoomed out (owner 2026-11-12 "ทำให้มันดูเล็กโดยซูมออก", free): `ZO=1.5 python3 tools/art/tw_ld_mock.py` → `tw_ld_mock_zo1.5.png` (the screen holds 1.5x more world, 585 x 1266 = a 3x phone at x2 instead of x3; layout, pedestals and HUD grow, characters / monsters / stone blocks keep their pixel size, so they look smaller and stay sharp). Side by side on the same phone: `tw_ld_zoom_cmp.png`.
Zoom out 2 (owner "ลองซูมออก 2"): `ZO=2` → `tw_ld_mock_zo2.png` (780 x 1688); pedestals now grow only by ZO^0.35 so they stay about the character's width. On a 3x phone zoom 2 = x1.5 per art pixel (not a whole number: pixels very slightly uneven), on a 2x phone x1. `tw_ld_zoom_cmp.png` = now / 1.5 / 2 side by side. At 2 the characters are small and the 6x3 board looks empty.
More slots (owner "เพิ่มช่อง", then "1.5 เพิ่มช่อง"): `GC` / `GR` env set the grid (default 6x3; cells fill the board's middle, half the slots filled on your side, a third on the opponent's): `ZO=1.5 GC=7 GR=4` (28 slots, Claude's pick: 8 columns at 1.5 crowd the katanas), `ZO=1.5 GC=8 GR=4`, `ZO=2 GC=8 GR=4` (32) → `tw_ld_mock_zo<ZO>_<GC>x<GR>.png`; side by side `tw_ld_grid_cmp.png`.
Only your board (owner "โชว์แต่จอตัวเอง" / "ดูว่าจะเหลือที่เยอะไหม"): `SOLO=1` drops the opponent's board (one bar at the top + a compact wave panel). `SOLO=1 ZO=1.5` = the board stretched to the buttons, 7x8 = 56 slots (`tw_ld_solo_zo1.5_7x8.png`); `FIT=0` keeps the two-board cell size (7x4) and marks the rest: 477 of 1266 px (~38% of the screen) empty (`tw_ld_solo_zo1.5_7x4_space.png`). Side by side: `tw_ld_solo_cmp.png`.

Playable step 1 (owner "ทำเลย", free): `dogcat.html` (one file, canvas 585x1266 scaled with hard pixels): the scene without units as the background (`BG=1 python3 tools/art/tw_scene.py` → `art/dogcat/bg.png` + `geo.json` = slot / loop / bench points), sprites from `tools/art/dogcat_assets.py` (`art/dogcat/units/` = the PixelLab south-east attack strips as they are, frame 0 = standing; `mobs/` = walk rows of 8 monsters halved; `sprites.json`). Rules in it: buy a shiba (3 gold, melee splash) or a corgi (4, long range blast) from two cards, tap a free pedestal to place; units attack the monster furthest along the loop inside their reach, damage on the hit frame; 3 same dog + star merge at once (1★→2★→3★); waves of 25 s, +3 gold per wave, 1 per kill, a boss wolf every 5 waves; monsters left when the timer ends cost HP by their number and stay (owner "ให้มอนค้างไปก่อน"); clearing early starts the next wave with +2 gold; HP 100 → 0 = game over screen. `?spd=N` speeds time; `window.DC` for tests. A scripted run with 8 units reached wave 13. Not in yet: bench, shop of 5 with reroll, drops + bag, path choice every 10 waves, cats, sound.

dogcat.html "more like a game" pass (owner "แก้ให้ดูเป็นเกมมากขึ้น", free): title card (หมา VS แมว, เริ่มเล่น; `?skip=1` or `DC.start()` skips it), a shop of 5 random cards (`G.shop`, `roll()`: free refresh each wave, สุ่มใหม่ = 1 gold; a bought card leaves an empty slot), wave banner ribbon (red "บอสมา!" on boss waves), coins that arc from a kill to the gold counter (which bumps), a purple portal where monsters appear, tap a placed dog = dashed reach ring + info card + ขาย (refund cost x 3^(star-1) - 1), red pulse in the last 5 s with monsters left and a red flash when HP is lost. `place(kind,slot)` unchanged for tests. Scripted run: no errors, wave 14.
dogcat.html bag (owner "ภาพช่วงล่างทำกระเป๋าเลย", free): the yard under the bench is now the bag panel (`drawBag`): a 7x4 grid of 56 px cells (`BAG`), a tray of 3 for drops (`TRAY`), a bin (`BIN`). Items `ITEM` (emoji icons on coloured shape tiles, placeholders until real art): bone 1x2 attack +10%, spiked collar 1x1 crit +6% (x2 damage), bowl 2x2 attack speed +18%, leash 1x3 reach +14%, gold tag L-shape +15% chance of an extra coin, treat 1x1 = items touching it +25% (gold corner mark). Drops: 7% per kill, always from a boss, only while the tray has room (`give`). Pointer drag with green / red landing cells, tap = turn 90°, drop on the bin = throw away; `buffs()` → `G.buf` used by damage, cooldown, reach (also the reach ring) and kill gold. The picked dog's card moved up onto the bench strip. Scripted Playwright run (programmatic placement + one real mouse drag): no errors, 6 dogs + 4 items reached wave 18 (14 before). Not in yet: bag expansion, item merging, bench, path choice every 10 waves.

Spritebench test (owner's zip `Spritebench_Game_Toolkit.zip`: an offline `index.html` + `spritebench.py` that packs frames into an equal-cell sheet + JSON + GIF; "เทสดูต่อเฟรมเนียนไหม"). Read first: no network calls, only Pillow; not copied into the repo. Run on our PixelLab clips (shiba 1★ idle, shiba 2★ attack, cat 2★ attack, 104 px cells): `--align source` reproduces the frames exactly (feet drift 0 px on the idle, 2-3 px in the attacks = the character's own steps, loop seam 0.5 px) = smooth; `--align feet` re-centres each frame on its opaque box, so anything that sticks out (sword swoosh, thrown fish, scarf) drags the body sideways: up to 9 px a frame for the shiba attack and 24 px for the cat's throw = visible shaking. It does not make in-between frames. Verdict: fine as a sheet packer for frames already on one canvas (our own tools do the same); never use its feet alignment on attack clips. Comparison: `art/incoming/dogcat/tooltest/spritebench_align_cmp.gif`.
Spritebench v2.1 (owner's second pack, `Spritebench_v2_1_Cross_FireMage_SizeFix.zip`; code read first, offline, Pillow only; not copied into the repo, its sample character is an anime character): re-running its README commands reproduces the shipped sheet pixel for pixel (18 frames, 1.5 s). `normalize_sheet.py` scales every pose round the feet to one body height from HAND-measured feet / head points (source heights 159-180 px → 172): works. `tween.py` "inserted frames" = the outlined weapon cut out and rotated between two poses over a shifted copy of the nearer body: on the sample the 3 inserted frames are near-copies of the neighbours and the body flips facing between them; no real in-betweens. Owner then "แก้ต่อให้ใช้ได้": Claude tried an automatic measurer (`sheet_fit.py`: feet = lowest rows, head = top of the thick body in a band above the feet) and DROPPED it: on the sample it was off by up to 14% (a raised staff with a thick head and fire over the face fool it), worse than the untouched sheet (±6%). Conclusion: for GPT sheets the head / feet points still have to be clicked by a person (the pack's index.html) or the poses made by PixelLab, which keeps one scale by itself; our dogs-vs-cats characters do not need this tool.
Spritebench bone mode (owner "แล้วกระดูกล่ะ"): `rig_render.py` read and run on the pack's own example `blade_scout.rig.json` (7 parts: body+legs, tail, head, scarf, upper arm → forearm+hand → wooden sword; 4 keys each) → 24 frames at 24 fps, 128x112, a smooth sword swing with the tail, head and scarf following (`art/incoming/dogcat/tooltest/spritebench_bone_test.gif`). This is real in-betweening (cut-out animation): parts rotate round pivots with a parent chain, nearest-pixel sampling. Limits: the character must first be split into parts by painting them in the editor, areas hidden behind a part need extra art or holes appear, rotated pixel art gets stair-step edges, and a part cannot change shape (no turning the body, no new face). Good for idles and weapon swings of a character seen from one side; it is the same idea as the DPS-style cut-out rig noted on 2026-11-07. Not tried on our own characters yet.
Own bone rig, first test (owner wants our own tool: bones + frame-by-frame VFX + posing bones over video frames; "ลองดูก่อน"): `tools/art/bone_rig.py` `rig(still, parts, n)` = parts cut by masks, pivot + parent + sine motions, nearest-pixel rendering, underlays against holes. Shiba 2★ idle from ONE still (frame 0 of his attack): tail wags ±13°, head sinks 1 px and tilts 1.5°, topknot sways ±7° → 16 frames `art/incoming/dogcat/rig/shiba2_idle.png`, `shiba2_idle_cmp.gif` (still vs rig); in the game through `dogcat_assets.py` (`shiba2.idle = 16`). Honest read: movement is small at 61 px and the tail is mostly hidden behind the body in this view; an attack cannot be rigged from this still (the sword is behind the shoulder, the arm is not a separate shape). Planned tool steps if the owner goes on: web editor to drag bones, video frame as an underlay to pose against, VFX pinned to a bone per frame.
Own pipeline, new plan (owner 2026-11-13: "การวาดเราให้ GPT วาด ใช้ของให้ถูกที่", "จะได้ทำได้ทุกแบบ ไม่ใช่แค่พิกเซล"): GPT draws, our tools design the poses, cut, line up, join frames, pin VFX; pixelising is an optional last step (painted 2D works too). `tools/art/pose_guide.py` draws colour-coded stick-figure pose sheets (4x2 cells of 384x512, one colour per limb, chibi proportions, nose = facing) + `*_poses.json` with every joint per cell (so the cutter knows feet / head / weapon positions): `slash8` (stand, lift, wind-up, swing, HIT at cell 5, follow-through, pull back, stand) and `run8` (run cycle made by formula; owner asked where to get a run from). Prompts `art/incoming/dogcat/pose/PROMPT_GPT_slash8.txt` / `_run8.txt` (attach the guide first, then the character picture). UNTESTED: whether GPT follows the stick figures and keeps face / size across 8 cells; waiting for the owner's first result. Lessons list from ~595 PixelLab credits given to the owner in chat (start frame decides everything; import our own views; side view steadier than diagonal; 8 frames = 1 credit; join sheets for one conversion; it adds unasked effects; video AI redraws characters but is fine for VFX; chat-pasted pictures are compressed).
Standard motion library (owner: "ไปเอาท่าจากพิกเซลมาเทียบ" → "เราเก็บแบบไว้ใช้ได้กับทุกตัว สแตนดาร์ด"): `tools/art/pose_lib.py` → `art/poses/std/`: 8 reference sheets cut from OUR OWN heroes' PixelLab moves (east = side view facing right; 8 numbered cells, 4x2, 1536x1024, one scale and ground line per sheet): idle, run, slash, roll, hurt, death (swordsman), shoot (archer), cast (mage); each with `<move>.json` (frames used, scale, ground) and `PROMPT_<move>.txt` (attach the sheet, then the character: "same poses, do not copy the look, no effects"); `index.png` overview, `pose_library_std.zip` to download. Replaces the stick-figure run guide (dropped); the colour stick-figure sheets (`pose_guide.py`) stay for moves we have no clip of. Known: the slash sheet shows the swooshes PixelLab drew into the source (the prompt says not to draw them); hurt has 5 source frames (cells repeat); death cells 5-8 are the same lying pose. Still untested with GPT.
Poses from 3D clips (owner: "ไปเอาท่าวิ่ง 3D จาก Mixamo มาเลยก็ได้ ... เลยให้ทำทูลขึ้นมา"): `tools/art/mixamo_pose.py <glb> <clip> --dir=<8 dirs> [--tilt=deg]` reads a GLB with a Mixamo skeleton in pure Python (no Blender): plays the clip, projects the joints from the chosen camera direction and draws the colour-coded stick figure (big chibi head, nearer limbs in the "front" colours) as an 8-cell sheet + 2D joints json → `art/poses/mixamo/`. Tested on our own `art/3d/storm_game.glb` (clips idle, run, shoot, reload, throw, roll, hit, die, die2): `run_east`, `run_south-east`, `run_south`, `throw_east` made; the run is a correct in-place cycle from every direction (the arms hold a rifle, Storm's clips are gun clips). So one 3D clip gives a move in all 8 directions for free. New Mixamo clips come as FBX: they need the owner's PC step `tools/blender/ls_step2_merge.py` (FBX → GLB) first; reading FBX directly is not built. Untested: GPT drawing from these sheets.

## Combo test with the Ozzbit hero template (2026-11-13)
Owner uploaded `template_free.zip` ("เอาลงดูทำการโจมตีคอมโบให้ได้"). Ozzbit Games male hero template, FREE version: 128 px cells, ~32 px colour-coded mannequin facing right, strips idle 10 / walk 10 / run 10 / jump 6 / fall 4 / fall_loop 3 / combo_1 3 / combo_1_end 4. Licence (`art/thirdparty/ozzbit_hero/LICENSE.txt`): personal, non-commercial only; changes allowed; no resale; credit "Ozzbit Games" required → fine as a test and a motion reference, NOT for a game we sell. More combo steps exist only in the paid PRO / DELUXE packs.
`combo.html` (root, free, no jobs): run / jump / 3-hit chain against dummies. Input buffer .28 s; the next hit starts when the current one ends or during the first 3 recovery frames. The chain is built from the one slash: hit 1 = combo_1; hit 2 = combo_1 backwards + longer lunge + the swoosh flipped upward (reads as a rising cut, pops the target up); hit 3 = hop (jump 1, 3, fall 1) + slam slash with the swoosh ×1.7, knock-back 150, hit-stop .12, shake, dust. Swoosh alone = pixels with min(r,g,b) ≥ 225 (`SW`). `STEP` holds the timing per hit.
Owner right after: "ทำไม่ได้ก็ปรับปรุงทูล" → the combo is now OUR OWN bone move, not the pack's frames. `art/poses/combo/sword_combo.json` = key poses (12 numbers: lean, front arm ×2, sword, back arm ×2, legs ×4, dx, air; time, ease in / out, which key is the hit) for 3 different hits: slash down, rising cut, jump slam. `combo.html` (button top right: 🦴 our bones / 🎞 Ozzbit frames, default bones) plays them with in-betweens every screen frame (`jointsOf`, `mix`, `ease`, `boneAtk`, `boneEnd`; chains blend from whatever pose he is in; run = the `run_poses` cycle in JS; sword trail `TR` from the tip's real path). `python3 tools/art/combo_guide.py` samples the same in-betweens into GPT pose sheets `art/poses/std/weapon/sword_combo{1,2,3}.png` + json + PROMPT. Known weak spots: samples are evenly spaced in time, so the hold at the end of a hit gives near-identical cells; the figure is a stick mannequin until a character is drawn on it (GPT sheet, or cut-out parts on the same joints = next step); no pose editor yet (poses are typed numbers).
Owner: "เอาดาบมาทำก็ได้ คลาส 1" → the swordsman on the same bones (free). `python3 tools/art/rig_parts.py` cuts `art/poses/combo/swordsman_parts.png` + json: head and torso (+ scarf, belt, the resting back arm) from his PixelLab east still, the sword from attack frame 6 mirrored. `combo.html` mode 🗡 (default; button cycles 🗡 / 🦴 / 🎞): `drawHero` with his own bone lengths `HL`; head only moves, torso is SHEARED by the lean (crisp pixels), sword rotates on the hand, front arm and both legs are pixel capsules (`limb`) in his colours because the still hides them; canvas ≈ 340 px tall so he shows 1:1 (`Z` = world scale). Preview `art/poses/combo/swordsman_rig_preview.png`. Weak spots: rotated sword edge is jagged, legs / arm are plain capsules (need drawn parts: thigh, boot, sleeve, glove), neck gap on big leans, trail is flat polygons (not his PixelLab swoosh).
Owner: "ไอ้ตัวชิบะที่ทำกระดูกมาทำได้ไหมท่านี้" → yes, free: the 2-star samurai shiba on the SAME move file. `rig_parts.py` also writes `art/poses/combo/shiba2_parts.png` (head + topknot, body + tail + robe from `pl2s` east still with the sheathed sword, breath puff, painted feet and shadow line removed; katana from attack_se frame 2 turned to point right). `combo.html` mode 🐕 (`HL1`: his own short bone lengths, legs 4 + 4 px, canvas ≈ 190 px tall; `setChar`, button cycles 🗡 / 🐕 / 🦴 / 🎞). Preview `art/poses/combo/shiba2_rig_preview.png`. Same move works on a 120 px human and a 60 px chibi dog = the point of shared moves. Weak spots as for the swordsman, plus: his big head hides most of the arm, a thin dark line left of the head (outline leftover), the lunge is short (`k` .3).
Owner: "ตอนนี้ไม่สวยเลยนะ" (the cut-out bone versions). Answer built at once, free: `combo.html` default mode ⭐ = the swordsman's OWN drawn frames (PixelLab clips already paid for) joined by our combo logic: `tools/art/combo_frames.py` → `art/poses/combo/swordsman_frames.png` (17 × 4 cells of 172: idle, run, attack, leap); `RSTEP`: hit 1 = attack 1-6 (side slash), hit 2 = attack 8-12 (overhead), hit 3 = leap 2-11 (jump slam), recovery attack 14-16; PixelLab's own swooshes / dust kept as drawn. Preview `swordsman_real_preview.png`. Lesson: cut-out bones on ONE still = fine for idle / small moves and for designing poses, NOT pretty for big attacks; pretty = drawn frames (PixelLab clips we own, or GPT drawing on our pose sheets), and our tool's job is joining / timing / chaining / hit feel. Button order: ⭐ real frames / 🗡 swordsman on bones / 🐕 shiba on bones / 🦴 stick figure / 🎞 Ozzbit.
**Meta AI video route works (owner 2026-11-13, "เอาชิบะดีกว่า ได้ใช้เลย").** Pack: `art/incoming/dogcat/meta/start_shiba2_magenta.png` (east still ×6 on flat magenta, 1280x720) + `PROMPT_META_shiba_combo.txt` (3-hit combo, still camera, no effects) → the owner's clip `shiba_combo_meta.mp4` (1280x720, 24 fps, 10 s). Meta REDREW him (bigger body, round topknot, sheath at the hip, turns to the camera in hit 2, drew thin slash arcs although told not to) but its version is steady through the whole clip and the motion is smooth and heavy. `python3 tools/art/meta_combo.py` (ffmpeg → magenta key → 1/3 size → hard alpha; frames picked by hand, list at the top) → `art/poses/combo/shiba_meta_frames.png` + json (40 cells 340x204: idle 7, hit 1 8, hit 2 9, hit 3 11, recovery 5; figure ≈ 120 px tall). `combo.html` default mode 🐕⭐ (`MSTEP` / `MEND`; no run frames: he slides on the idle with a bob). Nothing was removed from the frames (slash arcs kept). Open: he is 120 px here vs 61 px in dogcat.html (needs a ½ version or the game re-scaled), no run clip, his look differs from the PixelLab shiba (owner to choose which look is the real one). Also in that folder: sprite-SHEET prompts (`PROMPT_META_shiba_combo_sheets.txt`, 3 sheets × 8 frames) and a swordsman video pack (`art/incoming/action/swordsman/meta/`), both untested.
**Meta AI SHEET with the 3-view reference = best match so far (owner 2026-11-13 "ก็เอาแบบ 3 ทิศมาเป็นแบบสิ").** `art/incoming/dogcat/meta/ref3_shiba2_magenta.png` (south / east / north of the PixelLab 2-star shiba, ×5 on magenta) + prompts `*_ref3.txt` (say: one character from three sides, copy the design, do not enlarge / redesign). The owner's result `shiba_sheet_v1.png` (2000x1691, see-through, 3 rows × 7 figures, ~300 px tall): this time he IS our shiba (tufted topknot, robe pattern, sword on the back); Meta ignored the 4x2 layout and the no-effects rule (slash arcs, speed lines, gold slash, blue spin rings, gold ring; ground line under every figure) and mixed in front / back views. Rows: 1 = stand, turn, draw, thrust ×3, stand front; 2 = low guard ×2, dash ×2, low guard, rising gold slash, sword up (front); 3 = back view, side stand ×2, spin ×2, ring thrust, front stand. `python3 tools/art/meta_sheet.py` (per-frame cut rules because swords reach into the next figure; pinned by the ground line; ×0.4) → `art/poses/combo/shiba_sheet_frames.png` (cells 280x180, feet 120,172; index = row*7+col; frame 5 of row 1 is cut badly and unused). `combo.html` default mode 🐕📄 (`TSTEP`: hit 1 = 1,2,3,4 thrust; hit 2 = 8-12 dash + rising slash; hit 3 = 16-19 spin + ring; idle 15/16, run 9/10). Preview `shiba_sheet_combo.gif`. Nothing removed from the art. Weak: only 4-5 frames per hit (choppy next to the video's 8-11), idle is 2 near-identical frames.
Kept for later (owner 2026-11-13 "เก็บไว้"): Meta AI VFX sheet `art/incoming/dogcat/meta/vfx_sheet_v1.png` (+ the original `.webp`), 2000x1200, 3 rows × 5 frames, matching the shiba sheet's three hits: row 1 pale green-white slash arc (faint start, arc, big arc with sparkles, streaks, fade), row 2 gold diagonal slash (sparkles, thin, thick with stars, shatter, fade), row 3 blue swirl → big swirl → gold ring → gold / blue burst → fade. Not cut and not used yet; the pale row 1 will need care when the background is removed.
**Painted 2D (no pixels) route, first test (owner 2026-11-13 "ทูลเราทำ 2D ได้ไหม" → "ลองทำแบบไม่ใช้พิกเซลดูว่าทำท่าเดินได้ไหม").** New style reference = the owner's 4-class chibi picture (`art/incoming/darkchibi/REF_style_4classes.png`); swordsman 3-view `darkchibi/sword_v1/gpt_turn_v1.png` (made with `PROMPT_swordsman_3view_fromref.txt`), white cut `turn_cut.png`. Pixel tests of it: PixelLab A / B at body 128 (see the job log; B sharp but changed the eyes), free `chibi_px.py` at 142 / 284 (`free_px_*.png`, `sharp_cmp.png`): at 128 this costume is too detailed to stay crisp; 256 or no pixels is sharper. Two-picture GPT pixel-style prompt ready (`PROMPT_GPT_pixelstyle.txt` + `REF_pixel_style.png`), not run yet. Walk: `python3 tools/art/walk2d.py` = cut-out rig on the painted side view (cape sways, two legs from the one drawn leg with thigh / shin+boot, the sword-covered thigh filled with the trouser colour, upper body + arm + sword as one piece) → `walk2d_strip.png` (12 frames 250x280), `walk2d.gif`, `walk2d_parts.png`. Reads as a walk and stays 100% on model; smooth art turns without the jagged edges pixel parts get. Weak: the sword arm does not swing, the thigh is a flat block (hidden behind the tunic and sword most of the time), a ragged edge where the cape meets the back. Idea for many classes from ONE character: bare-handed base body + shared basic moves, weapons as separate pieces pinned to the hand, attack / skill sheets per weapon, recolours for class identity.
Chibi swordsman on the pixel map (owner 2026-11-13 "เอาตัวนี้ไปใส่ดูหน่อย"): `python3 tools/art/chibi_map_hero.py` cuts the 4 views of `pl2_b.png` → `art/td/map1/hero_chibi.png` (cells 131x141, feet 65,139, body 135 px) + `hero_chibi_half.png` (2x2 merge, 65x70, body ~67 px). `map.html?hero=chibi` (half size = the map hero's size) / `?hero=chibi2` (full size, about twice the map hero); stills only (front / 3-4 / side / back, mirrored for the left, a 2 px bob while moving), no walk frames yet. Comparison picture `darkchibi/sword_v1/map_compare.png` (old hero / half / full). At half size the face and trims blur into a few pixels; at full size he is crisp but too big for this map's props (the map would need showing closer, or the character generated at ~64 from the start). Owner's "ดาบเบี้ยว": PixelLab B drew the back-view blade wavy; a code redraw of the blade was tried and looked worse (smudges), so it was dropped: the blade will be replaced when weapons become separate pieces, or re-rolled.
Owner's reference clip (2026-11-13, `Recording_2026-11-13_095939.mp4`, not kept: 848x422, 8 panels) = the "one character, many weapons" game they meant: a white-haired chibi girl with a red scarf, the SAME body with a greatsword / gun / hammer / bow / lance / whip / bare hands, on a 3D lit HD-2D scene (grass, soft shadows, smooth non-pixel smoke and flashes). Measured: sprite ≈ 32-36 px tall (≈ 2 video px per art pixel), ~2 heads, very little costume detail. Owner then: "61 สิงั้น" (use 61 px like the shiba). Free shrink of the chibi swordsman to body 61 (`chibi_px.py` from the GPT pixel-style sheet and from the painting, and the 2x2-merged half) all come out muddy (`px61_free_cmp.png`): this costume has too many small parts for 61 without a redraw → PixelLab B at body 61 (1 credit, like the shiba at 66) or a simpler costume. NOT generated yet (no "เจน").
Map test also takes `map.html?hero=chibi36` (1:1, tiny next to this map's props) and `?hero=chibi36x2` (double-size pixels); `chibi_map_hero.py <src> <out name> <min size>` builds any of them. 256: not on the map (he would be ~4x the map hero); it is the size for a closer camera or as the master picture to shrink from.
Owner "ลองขยายแมพ ทำให้ตัวละครย่อลงครึ่งนึง": `map.html?hero=chibi128` = the 256 sheet halved (`chibi_map_hero.py … pl4_b256.png hero_chibi256 8000` → `hero_chibi256_half.png`, body ~125 art px, straight blades) with the camera twice as close (K doubled, ~200 map px tall on screen); one art pixel = half a map pixel, so he stands ~63 map px tall (right next to the props) but keeps his face and trims. Picture `darkchibi/sword_v1/map_zoom_compare.png` (zoomed / old half-size / the map's own hero). Trade: half as much map on screen, and the map's pixels show twice as big as the hero's.
Owner's second GPT pixel sheet, "8bit" (2026-11-13, `darkchibi/sword_v1/gpt_pixelstyle_v2.png`, 1920x1280 jpg, 3 views front / side / back): chunkier, flatter, darker violet hair, violet eyes, plain brown boots, simpler belt. Its blocks are ~9.05 px, so it is ALREADY ~130 px pixel art: `GPTPX_GRID=8.6,9.5 python3 tools/art/gptpx.py … v2_snap.png 16` snaps it to a true grid for FREE (209x131, `v2_snap_x6.png`; merge 40 tinted the trousers purple, 16 keeps the colours). No PixelLab needed for this kind of sheet. Fewer small parts than v1 = easier to redraw the same in every animation frame; v1 (PixelLab B 128 / 256) has the prettier face and trims.
Owner's third sheet "16 bit" (`gpt_pixelstyle_v3_16bit.png`, same layout and grid ~9 px as the "8bit" one): the same drawing with one more shade step in the hair, eyes and cloth; snapped free the same way (`v3_snap.png`, 211x129; `v2_v3_cmp.png`). GPT's "8 bit / 16 bit" words change the shading a little, not the size or the design.
Owner picked the 16-bit look for the map test ("เอา 16 bit ใส่แมพดู ปรับขนาดให้ดี"): `map.html?hero=chibi16` = `pl5_16bit_128.png` cut by `chibi_map_hero.py … hero_chibi16 2000` (3 views: front / side / back; cells 113x130, feet 56,128, body ~125), drawn at half a map pixel per art pixel with the camera twice as close (he stands ~63 map px, about a third of the screen height on a phone). Picture `darkchibi/sword_v1/map16.png`. Stills only; diagonals use the side or back view.
Prop references from the owner (2026-11-13): (1) a pixel-art pine set signed @KIANAMOSSER = another artist's finished work: style reference only, NOT stored in the repo and not to be used in the game; (2) a painted swamp-village sheet (`art/incoming/props_ref/swamp_village_painted.png`, 1024x1024, origin not stated: looks AI-made, owner to confirm it is theirs before it goes in a game) = not pixel art. Owner: whichever is not pixel art, turn it into pixel art first. Free `chibi_px.py` at 640 (`swamp_village_free_640.png`, close-up `swamp_village_cmp.png`): keeps the shapes, hard edges and a fixed palette, but it is the painting with pixels on it, noisy in the bark and stone; a real redraw = PixelLab B (1 credit per ≤512 px picture), not ordered. Also asked, not done yet: a wider map / zoomed-out view, and whether a 64 px character was generated (no: only 36 / 128 / 256 by PixelLab; ~61-67 only as free shrinks, muddy).
`map.html?hero=chibi64` = that 64 px version (`chibi_map_hero.py … pl6_16bit_64.png hero_chibi64 500 halo`; cells 52x66, feet 26,64) at one art pixel per map pixel in the normal wide view: same pixel size as the map and the monsters, as tall as the map's own hero. Pictures `darkchibi/sword_v1/map64.png`, `map64_close.png`. So far the best fit for THIS map without rebuilding it; `?hero=chibi16` (128, camera twice as close) is the sharper character on a coarser-looking map.
Owner chose 128 ("เอา 128 มาทำดีแล้ว") and asked for a bigger scene, zoomed out ("ทำให้ฉากใหญ่แล้วซูมออก"): `map.html?hero=chibi16` now sizes the view by the HERO's pixel: K = 2 x (screen px per hero px), default round(screen height / 640) → he is about a fifth of the screen (phone: 2 px per hero px, ~290 map px tall in view; 1080p PC: 2); `&px=1` = widest (hero ~11% of the screen, ~560 map px in view), `&px=3` = the earlier close view. Picture `darkchibi/sword_v1/map16_zoomout.png` (PC default / PC px=1 / phone). The map is still the old one drawn with double-size pixels; a real 2x-detail rebuild of `td_map.py` (3600x2200: props from the full-size sheets, tile textures laid at the same px size, cliffs / stairs scaled) is the open job. Owner then sent three pixel scene pictures with no words (a swamp dock, a river with a plank bridge, the @KIANAMOSSER pines again): chunky big-pixel painterly scenes; not saved (inline only), purpose to be confirmed.
Slash combo from a sketch (owner 2026-11-13, a rough animation sheet of a tiny swordsman, 29 frames: "อยากให้มันได้ท่าฟันประมาณนี้ทำไงได้บ้าง"): `art/poses/ref_sketch/slash_sketch_ref.png` = motion reference only (somebody else's sketch; the poses are followed, the drawing is not used). `python3 tools/art/sketch_pose_sheets.py` cuts it into numbered pose sheets `slash_A.png` (two side slashes, 8), `slash_B.png` (overhead chop into a spin, 8), `slash_C.png` (spin, dash, wide slash, 8), `slash_D.png` (follow-through and ready stance, 4) + `PROMPT_slash_<A-D>.txt` + `CHAR_swordsman16_side.png` (our 16-bit swordsman's side view ×6 on white). The owner attaches a sheet + the character in GPT / Meta; results come back as files and go through `meta_sheet.py`-style cutting into `combo.html`. Slash arcs are asked for (white-blue, inside the cell). First real test of "GPT follows a pose sheet" is still to come.

## Cleanup 2026-11-13

## bag.html — กระเป๋านักดาบ (2026-11-13)
Owner before sleeping: "ทำเกมอีกเกมให้ฉัน ให้คิดเองหมดเลย เกมแนว backpack ทำมาให้ฉันระหว่างฉันนอน ขอสนุกๆ". Built free (no PixelLab): one file `bag.html`, emoji items, hero = our chibi swordsman clips, enemies = our monster sheets.
Rules: 8x5 bag, 4x3 open at the start, more cells bought next to open ones (2 gold, +1 every 3). Shop of 5 (reroll 1, lock), tray for loose items, tap = select (info, rotate with the button or R, sell), drag to place, drop on the same item of the same star = merge (★2 x1.55, ★3 x2.3). 24 items: weapons (red; cooldown, stamina cost, crit / pierce / stun / burn / poison), defence (blue; block, max HP, thorns, dodge), food (green; heal, max HP, stamina regen), neighbour boosters (purple: +30% damage, -18% cooldown, +20% crit, burn, slow, 20% lifesteal, +1 star to every neighbour), utility (bomb at the start, piggy bank +1 gold a round, crown +15% all weapons). Battle is automatic (`tick`, `hit`): stamina 5 with 1/s regen, block soaks damage, poison stays and ignores block, burn fades, fatigue after 28 s, speed x1 / x2 / x4. 5 hearts, a boss every 5 rounds, round 15 = win then endless; best round saved (`bag.best`).
Enemy (`foe(round)`): HP (36 + 14r + r²) x1.5 for a boss, item budget (5 + 4.8r + 0.2r²) gold, +5% weapon damage a round, no neighbour bonuses; hero base HP 74 + 6r. `BG.sim(n)` = a bot that buys at random and puts items in the first free spot: median round 9, best 13, never clears 15, so a player who uses neighbours and merges has room to do better. NOT played by a human yet: the real difficulty for a person is unknown.
Not done: sound, enemy bag shown as a grid, item unlocks between runs, more enemy variety, a tutorial step.

PVFX Foundry 1.0 (owner 2026-11-13, upload `pvfx-foundry-thirteen-spritesheets.zip`, "มีอะไรน่าสนใจไหม"): 101 pixel-art effects (77 one-shots, 24 loops; arcane, explosions, fire, holy, ice, impacts, lightning, melee, nature, projectiles, shadow, smoke, status, ui, water, wind), two tiers × two layouts, exact per-frame metadata. Licence: CC0 1.0 for the sprite sheets and manifests (commercial use allowed, no credit required; the pack says every pixel is first-party; made with the MIT pixelfx toolkit we already use). Kept: the `lit` tier, `grid` layout only → `art/pvfx/<group>/<effect>/{sprite-sheet.png, manifest.json}` (frames[].sheet = x, y, width, height; duration per frame; `pivot`), licence + notices in `vendor/pvfx-foundry/`, overview `art/pvfx/preview_all.png`. Best fits: melee (sword-combo, axe-smear, hammer-smash, shadow-claw, spear-thrust), impacts (crit-hit, ground-slam, hit-spark, parry, blood-splat), status (burning-aura, poisoned, frozen-chill, bleeding, electrified, stunned), holy (barrier-block, holy-heal), explosions, projectiles. Not wired in yet.

- 15 waves, golem boss on 5 / 10 / 15, 20 lives (boss leak = 5), speed x1-x3, full-screen button, title + end cards, best wave in localStorage `td.best`. `window.TD` (`G`, `build`, `upgrade`, `startWave`, `step`, `bot()`); `?skip=1` skips the title.

Each side defends its own copy of the map; no waves: creeps come by themselves and grow with time (`duelStep`, wave = 1 + t / 24 s), gold arrives every 10 s (income, starts 20), kills pay 60%. The new part: a row of 6 monster buttons (`SEND`: cost, income gained) sends that monster to the OTHER side and raises your income for good. First gate to 0 lives loses.
  - vs bot: a second board `BOT` runs the same `step` (global `G` is swapped; `hud` / `say` / `snd` only act for `ME`), `botAI` builds by `ST.bot`, upgrades and sends. A player who only defends beat it in ~5 min in the fast sim.
  - online: PeerJS from unpkg (browser to browser, public broker), 4-digit room code (`hostRoom` / `joinRoom`, peer id `lastsignal-td-<code>`). Only `{t:'start',stage}`, `{t:'send',k}`, `{t:'lives',n}` travel; leaving = the other wins. Protocol tested with a mocked Peer between two pages (start, send, lose / win); the REAL connection could not be tested from the cloud session (unpkg is blocked there): the owner must try it with two devices.
